# v5.B.1 decision log

Author decisions, newest last. "Open" items need the author before they bind. B1–B15 (`docs/v5b/design_v0.md` §2 on
`claude/v5b-core`) are draft inputs until re-confirmed here. Review findings are in `docs/architecture/review_v0.md`.

| # | Date | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| V1 | 2026-10-13 | Branch `v5b1`, empty history, is the main branch and the GitHub default (confirmed `origin/HEAD → v5b1`) | decided |
| V2 | 2026-10-13 | The CORR2X network evolves into **F3Z** ("Freeway, 3 zones"). Supersedes the name F4R1M proposed the same day | decided |
| V3 | 2026-10-13 | **Action granularity: per-CAV commands** (accelerate / hold / brake in ±5 km/h steps). Lane/segment-level CAV targets were tried earlier and failed (author's experience, not a re-checked log). Zone-level agents also "almost failed" (author) | decided |
| V4 | 2026-10-13 | **No control (NC):** no speed limit beyond the network maximum, no speed set for any vehicle, **ramp meters off (always green)**. NC is not used in training; it is used in evaluation (the D5.8 denominator, extremes) | decided |
| V5 | 2026-10-13 | **HDV lane limits** as in v5: set the lane's maximum speed; HDVs comply partly (speed-factor spread). Full compliance is not wanted: the noise is part of the problem. **CAVs are kept outside lane limits by speed mode 95** (verified 2026-10-13 in SUMO 1.27.1, single vehicle, lane max 72 km/h: `setSpeed(108)` reaches 72 under the default mode 31, 108 under mode 95; mode 95 kept the safe gap behind a slower leader) | **superseded by V15** (no lane limits); the speed-mode finding is kept for reference |
| V6 | 2026-10-13 | **Invalid run:** more than 2 collisions, or any teleport (> 0). A collision can happen through HDV non-compliance | decided; an invalid run counts as **that arm's failure on that seed** (never dropped for all arms) |
| V7 | 2026-10-13 | **Multi-agent API: PettingZoo.** Direction: **each CAV is an agent** | decided; trainer: see V17 |
| V8 | 2026-10-13 | **No pause gate.** Controllers are always active; they must learn when to start controlling, before the jam forms. (The answers given for a gate: one shared rule, no pause in a full jam, release CAVs / lanes to max / meters green while paused, are kept only in case a gate is reconsidered) | decided |
| V9 | 2026-10-13 | **Retire the word "demand"** in the design: the controller must not be told the inflow; it learns from what it measures | decided; the replacement term is **inflow** (vehicles arriving at the network) |
| V10 | 2026-10-13 | **New metric: energy use** (ICE and electric), including acceleration and braking; the author's most important metric for speed harmonisation. From SUMO if available, else a published model | decided: **energy per vehicle-km per cohort plus the network total** (kWh), with power traces logged for diagnosis; open: the model; its role: see V16 |
| V11 | 2026-10-13 | **Hard braking measured on CAVs** | decided; open: the threshold (3–4 m/s² proposed). HDV hard braking: placeholder only, not measured for now (it cannot be reported in reality) |
| V12 | 2026-10-13 | **No step 0 run now.** Gather information and design first; NC is defined for evaluation later | decided |
| V13 | 2026-10-13 | **Network maximum speed 120 km/h**, a variable that may change later (e.g. 100 km/h) | **superseded by V30** |
| V14 | 2026-10-13 | **Separate Python environments** for v5.B.1 and v6, accepting duplicated disk and memory use | decided |
| V15 | 2026-10-13 | **No variable speed limits and no HDV lane limits.** The system is CAV commands only: CAVs keep a steady speed for the circumstances (fewer decelerations and accelerations) and so steady the traffic around them. A deployed system without VSL gantries is cheaper. HDVs are not measured by the system | decided. **Ramp meters dropped too** (may be added later): CAV commands are the only actuator. Evaluation covers **all vehicles**: HDVs add non-linearity and realism, and harm to them shows in the total cost |
| V16 | 2026-10-13 | **Headline = a mix of energy, delay and CAV hard braking**, designed so that energy cannot be won by slowing traffic: the author's earlier harmonisation results cost the CAVs too much speed. The trade-off is designed in from the start | decided: **form A, generalised cost** (value of time × delay + energy price × kWh + a cost per CAV hard brake, per vehicle-km, over all vehicles); the training reward uses the same mix. Open: the values and their sources, the delay guard x %, the sensitivity rows |
| V17 | 2026-10-13 | **Trainer: our own shared-parameter PPO (CleanRL style) on a standard PettingZoo `ParallelEnv`**, built to be replaceable (`docs/architecture/adr/ADR-001-trainer.md`: contracts, verification ladder, switch triggers, fallbacks RLlib → TorchRL/BenchMARL → SB3+SuperSuit) | decided |
| V18 | 2026-10-13 | **Metric definitions** (cohort, reference time, cut-off, window, stop-and-go) are adjusted after the energy decision | pending V16 |
| V19 | 2026-10-13 | **Comparators:** with no VSL and no meters, the best tuned proven controller for the D5.8 bar is a published **rule-based CAV controller** (e.g. CAV speed harmonisation, jam absorption; candidates from the literature, plus the archived MRG3 staircase / PI rules) | decided; open: which ones (literature check) |
| V20 | 2026-10-13 | **SI units and euro throughout** (km/h, m, s, L, kWh, €); no US customary units | decided |
| V21 | 2026-10-13 | **Energy prices are Irish retail** (author, 2026-10-13): Euro 95 petrol €1.98/L, diesel €2.15/L, household electricity incl. home EV charging €0.4042/kWh. These are among the highest in Europe and are chosen deliberately; they are retail prices incl. taxes (perceived cost), so a lower-price / resource-cost sensitivity row is reported alongside (CLAUDE.md: no proxy headlines) | decided; source: author statement, date of prices to record |
| V22 | 2026-10-13 | **Network inspired by Dublin's M50** | decided; open: consistency with the MnDOT inflow shape, the 120 km/h maximum and US values of time |
| V23 | 2026-10-13 | **Fleet:** CAVs are electric or hybrid; HDVs about 80 % ICE (petrol and diesel mixed in), the rest electrified (**shares superseded by V42**). In a deployment, CAVs could report their energy over V2X, HDVs cannot: HDV energy is **estimated** with the model, and the report says why | decided; open: exact shares, hybrid model availability in SUMO |
| V24 | 2026-10-13 | **Energy totals per scenario** are reported (whole-scenario kWh and € saved) next to per-vehicle-km values, because under money weights the energy share of the cost is small | decided |
| V25 | 2026-10-13 | **Hard braking is a constraint**, not a priced cost (no accepted price exists; a price made crawling optimal) | decided; open: threshold and tolerance y % |
| V26 | 2026-10-13 | **Command execution:** a CAV command ramps over 2 s with `slowDown(v_target, 2 s)`, then holds `v_target` (a plain `setSpeed` step brakes at the vType decel, about −4.5 m/s², in one step). The same execution layer serves the DRL and every comparator | decided; hold mechanism to verify (slowDown releases to the desired speed after its duration) |
| V27 | 2026-10-13 | **Communication: V2V and V2X** (CAV-to-CAV and zone messages, i.e. infrastructure-side zone units). **No loop detectors** for now | decided; open: whether a CAV's own onboard sensors (speed, radar gap and closing speed to its leader, which may be an HDV) are allowed |
| V28 | 2026-10-13 | **HBEFA4 truck curve** (fuel per km falling with speed) is checked against a second source before trucks enter the reward | checked (`docs/research/truck_energy_check_v0.md`): a fitting artefact (zero v²/v³ terms in most HDV classes); recommended road-load + Willans model for trucks. Author to decide. HBEFA4 has no HEV car class (PHEV pairs only) |
| V29 | 2026-10-13 | **Headline (form A):** generalised cost in € per vehicle-km over all vehicles = value of time × delay + energy price × energy, divided by a **fixed denominator** (the inflow's vehicle-km), ≥ 10 % vs NC under D5.8. Guards: delay not worse than NC by more than x %; hard braking as a constraint (V25). Energy (kWh) and delay are also reported on their own, plus scenario totals (V24) | decided; open: x %, the Irish value of time uplifted to 2026 € (TII PAG growth + CPI) |
| V30 | 2026-10-13 | **Network maximum speed 100 km/h** (as on the M50); still a variable | decided |
| V31 | 2026-10-13 | **Inflow is synthetic.** Sweep a range of inflow levels, then narrow to the band where control is worth it | decided; the narrowing rule must be fixed before data and use non-learning arms only (see protocol) |
| V32 | 2026-10-13 | **Infrastructure comparators (research question):** VSL as on the M50 (eMOS-like) with realistic, low compliance, and **VSL + average-speed (section) cameras** with high compliance. Can they beat the CAV controllers? Author's experience: drivers on the M50 ignore the VSL and stop-and-go waves form (not a re-checked measurement). Our system stays CAV-only (V15) | decided; open: compliance values and their sources |
| V33 | 2026-10-13 | **CAV powertrains:** about 90 % BEV, about 10 % petrol PHEV | **superseded by V42** |
| V34 | 2026-10-13 | **Truck energy:** road-load + Willans model (`docs/research/truck_energy_check_v0.md`); COPERT and the TII OGV fuel function as sensitivity rows; trucks capped at 90 km/h | decided |
| V35 | 2026-10-13 | **HDV hybrids (HEV+PHEV) are modelled as the petrol PHEV pair** (`HBEFA4/PC_PHEV_petrol_Euro-6d_(P)` / `_(El)`); no diesel hybrids. HDV mix from CSO stock (June 2026): diesel 48.7 %, petrol 33.5 %, hybrids 12.9 %, BEV 4.8 % | decided; open: how a PHEV is split between its two modes; truck share |
| V36 | 2026-10-13 | **Agreed:** CAVs get a calibrated ACC base model, and a human-only NC reference row is reported; a command is `slowDown` over 2 s, then `setSpeed` to hold, released (`setSpeed(-1)`) when the CAV leaves the zone; onboard sensors (own speed, radar gap and closing speed to the leader) are allowed; the hard-brake threshold is 3 m/s²; per-km metrics use the fixed denominator | decided |
| V37 | 2026-10-13 | **Delay guard x = 2 %**: harmonisation must not cost commuters noticeable time | decided |
| V38 | 2026-10-13 | **PHEVs use SUMO's built-in PHEV pair as is**; no own or derived hybrid model. Each PHEV gets one mode per trip; the electric-mode share is a labelled assumption | decided; open: the share |
| V39 | 2026-10-13 | **VSL vs VSL + average-speed cameras:** the compliance difference (low under VSL alone, high with section control) is the stated rationale when asked whether such infrastructure would beat the CAV controllers; compliance levels are labelled assumptions (author's M50 experience, not a measurement) | decided |
| V40 | 2026-10-13 | **Heavy-vehicle share about 7.5 %**, supported by TII: HGV 7.5 % of AADT on M50 J5–J6 (2025) and OGV1+OGV2 7.76 % of the 2016 motorway mix (PAG T6.11.32). These are daily / annual shares, not AM-peak shares (assumption) | decided |
| V41 | 2026-10-13 | **First baseline: an all-HDV fleet with real-world desired speeds.** Trucks, buses and big vehicles 90 km/h; over 50 % of cars at 100 km/h when traffic permits; a few per 1,000 cars at 120–150 km/h. Powertrains: diesel, petrol, electrified and hybrids (HEV+PHEV). The controlled road keeps the same powertrain proportions; the experiment asks: what if the same vehicles are driven as CAVs instead of by humans (energy, total cost)? Affects V31 | decided; open: see the questions of 2026-10-13 (which vehicles become CAVs, fleet year, the full car speed distribution, which baseline the D5.8 bar uses) |
| V42 | 2026-10-13 | **Fleet = the 2025 new-car sales mix** (SIMI, 2 Jan 2026): petrol 25.11 %, diesel 17.09 %, HEV 22.48 % + PHEV 14.82 % (modelled as the petrol PHEV pair, V35), BEV 18.89 %. Heavy vehicles 7.5 % on top (V40, 90 km/h). This is a near-future fleet, not today's M50 traffic (label it so). **CAVs = 50 % of the electrified + hybrid cars**: about 28 % of cars; CAV powertrains about 34 % BEV / 66 % PHEV; HDV cars about 59 % ICE. The powertrain mix is identical in the baseline and the controlled scenario | decided |
| V43 | 2026-10-13 | **Car desired speeds (HDV):** 55 % at 100 km/h, 44.7 % at 100–120 km/h, 0.3 % at 120–150 km/h; heavy vehicles 90 km/h. Labelled assumptions | decided |
| V44 | 2026-10-13 | **D5.8 bar baseline:** ≥ 10 % vs the **all-HDV** fleet, AND the controller must beat the **same CAVs on plain ACC without commands** (control, not automation, must carry the gain). Both rows are reported | decided |
| V45 | 2026-10-13 | **"Worth controlling" threshold g = 10 %** (V31): an inflow level is in the band when the best non-learning arm or the informed oracle cuts the generalised cost by ≥ 10 % vs the all-HDV baseline | decided |
| V46 | 2026-10-13 | **PHEV electric-mode share 50 %** (V38), a labelled assumption | decided |
| V47 | 2026-10-13 | **CAV target floor 50 km/h** (author: below 50 km/h the jam cannot be optimised); the ceiling is 100 km/h; on zone entry the target starts at the current speed rounded to 5 km/h. Whether the floor should be higher stays open: the tool checks log how often targets sit at the floor | decided; open: a higher floor |
| V48 | 2026-10-13 | **No clock in any arm's observation**: controllers learn when to act from measurements, not time of day | decided |
| V49 | 2026-10-13 | **Episode:** 15 min warm-up (fills the road; past controllers were confused by an empty road followed by high inflow), 2.5 h scored window, drain capped at 30 min | decided (initial values) |
| V50 | 2026-10-13 | **V2V:** range 300 m (DSRC range in NHTSA's V2V fact sheet); a CAV hears all CAVs in range. The observation keeps the 5 nearest CAVs ahead plus the count and mean speed of all heard within 300 m ahead. "3 cars" was not a standard and is dropped | decided |
| V51 | 2026-10-13 | **Planned profiles between zones (B4) postponed**; zones share traffic summaries | decided |
| V52 | 2026-10-13 | **The reward and the controller type stay open to change** during the exploratory phase, because the reward is where such systems usually fail. Other controller types may be adopted (ADR-001 keeps the trainer replaceable). Every variant goes into the ledger; the candidate, reward included, is frozen before the confirmatory run | decided |
| V53 | 2026-10-13 | **No charging-loss factor for now**: electricity is priced at the battery value. It may be added later. First prove the setup works | decided |
| V54 | 2026-10-13 | **Truck value of time** = Irish work VTT × 1.03 occupancy, uplifted to 2026 € | decided |
| V55 | 2026-10-13 | **VSL comparator signs** change every 60 s: the zone's mean CAV-probe speed over the last minute, rounded up to the next 10 km/h, capped at 100 km/h | decided; open: 10 km/h steps vs 5 |
| V56 | 2026-10-13 | **Zone positions** Z1–Z3 kept for now, but open to change if that helps the controller learn. Any change applies to every arm and is frozen before the confirmatory run | decided |
| V57 | 2026-10-13 | **External research runs** on the Claude Console Workbench (author budget $200): Opus 5.5 + web search for source research, Fable 5.1 without tools for second-angle reviews of fully described ideas. Never codebase work. Prompts and pasted answers live in `docs/research/external/`; Claude Code spot-checks citations before use | decided |
| V58 | 2026-10-13 | **Redesign the road so the first jam forms inside the zones** (protocol A26 option b). The desk estimate showed that the 2-lane section after merge 2, downstream of every zone, binds first | decided; open: which redesign (options in the reply of 2026-10-13) |
| V59 | 2026-10-13 | **Realism FAIL rule (A1):** at most 2 road variants and 6 setting changes in all, each on fresh seeds, then P0 KILL; never continue with a disclosed realism FAIL | decided |
| V60 | 2026-10-13 | **ACC candidate A3 allowed (A11):** an IDM-based ACC without driver noise (Kesting et al. 2008 [VERIFY]) may be selected if A0–A2 fail, although it departs from "SUMO ACC" | decided |
| V61 | 2026-10-13 | **Beating plain-ACC CAVs (B1) means at least 5 % better** in generalised cost (A7), not merely a significant improvement | decided |
| V62 | 2026-10-13 | **Seed range 7,401,000–7,401,999 approved** for re-rounds (A16c) | decided |
| V63 | 2026-10-13 | **The non-learning adaptive scheduler is dropped from P2 (A31)**, disclosed; the headroom figure is relabelled accordingly | decided |
| V64 | 2026-10-13 | **Road redesign, option 1 (V58):** the lane drop moves to the end. Layout:<br>• 3 lanes 0–3,000 m (Z1 2,000–3,000);<br>• merge 1 at 3,000–3,250 (OR1 joins; auxiliary lane continues as X1);<br>• 3 lanes 3,250–4,500 (Z2 3,500–4,500);<br>• merge 2 at 4,500–4,750 (OR2 joins; auxiliary lane continues as X2);<br>• 3 lanes 4,750–6,000 (Z3 5,000–6,000);<br>• **lane drop 3→2 at 6,000**;<br>• 2 lanes 6,000–9,250.<br>Desk estimate (lane capacity 1,600–2,000 veh/h): the drop at the end of Z3 binds first at about 61–76 % of the B13 peak; the next tightest section (3 lanes after merge 2) only at about 91–114 %. The queue then grows back through Z3, merge 2, Z2 and merge 1. The name stays F3Z | decided |
| V65 | 2026-10-13 | **All remaining protocol [AUTHOR] items take the protocol's recommendation** (A2–A6, A8–A10, A12–A15, A16 a/b/d, A17–A25, A27–A30, A32–A35). Items written for the old layout (A6, A13, A26, A30) are re-read for the V64 layout. A1 = V59, A7 = V61, A11 = V60, A16c = V62, A31 = V63 | decided |
| V18 | 2026-10-13 | (update) **Metric definitions** are closed by the protocol's metric section (protocol A35) | decided |
| V22 | 2026-10-13 | (update) The **M50-inspired network vs the earlier MnDOT-based values** is answered as a disclosed mismatch: the inflow is synthetic (V31), and values and prices are Irish (V21, V29) (protocol A35) | decided |
| V42 | 2026-10-13 | (update, protocol A13 via V65) The five SIMI shares sum to 98.39 %; they are **renormalised**: petrol 25.52 %, diesel 17.37 %, HEV 22.85 %, PHEV 15.06 %, BEV 19.20 % of cars. CAVs ≈ 28.6 % of cars; HDV cars **60.0 % ICE** (was "about 59 %") | decided |
| V59 | 2026-10-13 | (update) **Six setting changes after the original plant** (k ≤ 7 counting the original); a plant-model variant may be a road or driver-model variant (recommended; not objected to) | decided |
| V61 | 2026-10-13 | (update) **Reading (a):** paired median gain vs B1 ≥ 5 % **and** its 95 % CI excludes 0 | decided |
| V66 | 2026-10-13 | **Protocol P0–P2 v1 approved by the author** (`docs/research/protocol_p0p2_v1.md`). Binding before seed 7,400,000; changes only by dated addenda that never relax a bound | decided |
| V67 | 2026-10-13 | **Disclosure: GUI demo before P0** (author request). Informal runs of the V64 layout on non-project seed 1 (no project seed used) at 70 / 80 / 90 % of the B13 peak, B0 and B1, with a compressed 70-min triangle and the scratch generator `make_demo.py` (not project code). Only SUMO summary lines were read: mean time loss per vehicle about 11 s (70 %), 31–36 s (80 %), 82–90 s (90 %). They are **not data** for any protocol check; P0 calibration and checks run only on the approved seeds | recorded |
| V68 | 2026-10-13 | **More realistic spacing (author):** 5 km of plain motorway between the zones; each control zone stays 1 km, directly before its trouble spot. Layout (supersedes V64's positions, not its order):<br>• 3 lanes 0–3,000 (Z1 2,000–3,000);<br>• merge 1 3,000–3,250 (OR1 in, X1 out);<br>• 3 lanes 3,250–9,000 (Z2 8,000–9,000);<br>• merge 2 9,000–9,250 (OR2 in, X2 out);<br>• 3 lanes 9,250–15,000 (Z3 14,000–15,000);<br>• **lane drop 3→2 at 15,000**;<br>• 2 lanes 15,000–18,250.<br>Lane counts and flows are unchanged, so the desk capacity table and the critical levels are unchanged; queue storage and travel times grow. Protocol Addendum G1 (geometry) records the new positions before any project seed | decided |
| V67 | 2026-10-13 | (update) Two more informal GUI-demo runs on the V68 layout, non-project seed 1, B1: mean time loss about 45 s (80 %) and 116 s (90 %). Not data | recorded |
| V69 | 2026-10-13 | **Disclosure: pre-P0 pilot (author request).** 136 runs on non-project seeds 1–5 (no project seed): F3Z V68 with B0 and B1 at λ = 0.55–1.00 (5 seeds each), plus two layout variants (a plain lane end instead of the zipper; 3 km between zones) at λ = 0.7/0.8/0.9 (3 seeds). No controller. It follows protocol §2.2–2.4 where cheap, with pilot-only choices: masses, induction loops every 250 m for evaluation, the delay proxy SUMO timeLoss + departDelay. Purpose: plant behaviour, data correctness, layout options. **Not protocol data.** No PASS rule or bound may change because of it; a layout change needs a decision and a dated protocol addendum before project seeds | recorded |
| V70 | 2026-10-13 | **Capacity drop (pilot O1):** before P0, adopt a human-driver model setting taken from **published** SUMO studies that reproduce a capacity drop; chosen from literature, never tuned on our data (protocol principle 10). Recorded as a dated protocol addendum before seed 7,400,000 | decided; literature search pending (prompt X04) |
| V71 | 2026-10-13 | **Zones on the drop's approach (pilot O2):** Z3 0–1 km before the drop (14–15 km), Z2 3–4 km before (11–12 km), Z1 6–7 km before (8–9 km, ending at merge 2). Merges stay as road features. Supersedes the zone positions of V68 (the road itself is unchanged); protocol addendum before seed 7,400,000 | decided |
| V72 | 2026-10-13 | **CAV desired speed = that of the driver it replaces (pilot O3)**, so automation itself does not add delay (supersedes protocol A13 (iii)) | decided; open concern raised 2026-10-13: automated vehicles exceeding the 100 km/h limit |
| V73 | 2026-10-13 | **Pilot extension:** test higher inflows (λ > 1.0) to see where and whether the bottlenecks show a capacity drop. Non-project seeds, not data (V69 rules) | decided |
| V72 | 2026-10-13 | (update, supersedes the decision above) **CAVs obey the 100 km/h limit** (desired speed 100 km/h, speed factor 1.0), as in protocol A13 (iii). The part of the B1 delay that comes from automation obeying the limit is reported openly (B1 − B0 rows). The B1s pilot variant (CAVs keeping the driver's speed) is report-only | decided |
| V73 | 2026-10-13 | (update) Higher-inflow pilot done (27 runs, non-project seeds): no capacity drop at any λ up to 1.5 (drop discharge ≈ 1,920 veh/h/lane, constant); merges never independent bottlenecks; above λ ≈ 1.05 the queue fills the road to the entry. Not data | recorded |
| V71 | 2026-10-13 | (update) Recorded in protocol Addendum Z1 together with V72 (b) (no protocol change for V72: §2.3 already has CAVs at 100 km/h) | recorded |
| V74 | 2026-10-13 | **Main claim = safety and smoothness** (wave damping), as in earlier CAV studies. The generalised cost (V29) becomes a reported, secondary result, to be claimed later only if the framework proves itself. Reason (written before any project data): the pilot (V69, not data) showed about 85–95 % of delay is unavoidable queueing at the drop, so delay-based gains are capped without a capacity drop. The 2 % delay guard (V37) stays | decided; open: the exact primary metric(s); requires protocol v2 before seed 7,400,000 |
| V75 | 2026-10-13 | **Incident regime added:** a temporary lane blockage as a pre-registered regime | decided; open: location, duration, timing |
| V76 | 2026-10-13 | **Lane-choice commands for CAVs open now (S5)**, in addition to the ±5 km/h speed commands (extends V3). Comparators get matching lane rules; the fair-DRL rule (same actuators) applies | decided; open: action form |
| V74 | 2026-10-13 | (update) **Two co-primary measures**, each needing ≥ 10 % vs the baseline under D5.8, with a multiplicity correction for testing two:<br>• **smoothness** (speed fluctuation in the jam area);<br>• **safety** (time spent at time-to-collision < 1.5 s, plus hard-braking events).<br>Delay guard 2 % (V37) kept | decided; exact definitions in protocol v2 |
| V75 | 2026-10-13 | (update) **Incident default:** one of the three lanes blocked for 15 min at about 13 km (between Z2 and Z3), starting during the rising peak; tested at 2–3 inflow levels | decided |
| V76 | 2026-10-13 | (update) **Action every 2 s per CAV: speed {−5, 0, +5 km/h} × lane {keep, left, right} = 9 combinations**; SUMO's lane-change safety decides whether a requested change happens now | decided |
| V77 | 2026-10-15 | **Scope narrowing (author):** "far too many decisions … the project is far too broad"; no project that needs ten people. Protocol v2 (A36–A68) is **on hold, not answered**. A research sweep (beyond the earlier literature) proposes the **top three focused directions** balancing recent research and impact. Dropping DRL is **under consideration**: the plant is too complex, and the author lacks the experience, people, tools and real data. Changing D5.8 (learning-based claim) needs an explicit decision | decided (narrowing); open: the direction, DRL yes/no |
| V78 | 2026-10-15 | **Direction sweep v0 options A/B do not fit the title** (author): they are measurement studies, not "adaptive driving algorithms". A new short search for **algorithm-first** options under the fixed title: the contribution must be an adaptive driving algorithm, with a simple testbed. The merged "A+B" idea (gap that adapts to waves and cut-in risk) is a seed candidate only | decided; open: the direction, DRL yes/no (V77) |
| V79 | 2026-10-16 | **Develop #1 and #2** of the algorithm-first sweep (`docs/research/direction_sweep_v1_algorithm_first.md`): #1 a leader-adaptive (conformal) RSS safety filter behind real leaders; #2 decision-calibrated eco-approach at actuated signals (Hamburg data). Re-check the approach first. **Kill tests are sized at machine speed** (hours of compute, plus download and author time stated separately), not "2 weeks" | decided; open: DRL drop / D5.8 replacement (V77), each option's parameters |
| V80 | 2026-10-16 | **Two tiers (author).** (1) A **one-page screen** per option on throw-away data: about 5 rules, fixed and committed before data; no claim; outcome "continue" or "stop". (2) A **confirmatory protocol** only after a screen passes, based on the drafts in `docs/research/protocols/` (not binding). The author: "Do the screen". The claim-rule items (D5.8 replacement, DRL drop) stay open until the confirmatory stage | decided |

| V141 | 2026-10-21 | **Gate outcome: work-zone plant plus a fixed actuator** (author choice on the V140 results; the V133 fallback). **Plant WZ:** 3 upstream lanes (entry 200 m, meter approach and storage 600 m to the signal, 200 m to the drop), a 3→2 zipper lane drop, a 2-lane work-zone bottleneck of 400 m, then 200 m to the exit; 23 m/s; same edge ids 1-5; meter across all 3 lanes. Incidents: lane closure = stopped blocker on lane 4_1 inside the 2-lane bottleneck (capacity really falls to one lane); slowdown = edge 4 at 5 m/s. **Actuator fix (T0):** a release-tracking signal. It counts vehicles crossing the stop line and keeps them on the commanded cumulative count: green ≥ 6 s, then amber 5 s and red ≥ 2 s once the count is ahead; green again once 3 vehicles (1 per lane) are owed; the owed count is capped; it rests in green at or above R_REST. **Seeds:** calibration on 7,430,010-019 (stage 1, same 54-configuration grid and selection rule); tool checks on 7,430,100-149 with the gate's check structure. **Criteria unchanged,** with one adaptation forced by the geometry: T1b's field QDF range [1,770, 2,330] applies **per lane** of the 2-lane bottleneck. Demand levels and constant-rate grids are scaled to the new capacity (q_near = calibration pre-queue flow, rounded to 50 veh/h; T1f grid 0.6-1.0 × QDF; T0 rates 0.4/0.6/0.8 × QDF). | V152 | 2026-10-21 | **Where RL can still add value: synthesis v0** (author: "understand how the DRL/RL could still work and see what we can bring new"). Inputs: six full-text thesis reviews (`docs/research/lit_reviews/`, page-cited, key claims spot-checked) and a web sweep of about 45 papers. Result: `docs/research/rl_value_synthesis_v0.md`. Findings: no thesis or paper found calibrates or varies the capacity drop or beats a tuned feedback controller on mobility. On BN4 the best RL equals ALINEA at 40 % AVs, with lane changes off (Vinitsky et al. 2023, checked in the arXiv full text, lines 623 and 821). RL's documented value is adaptation and robustness, safety and responsiveness (I-24 MARVEL), CAV smoothing (non-RL controllers competitive) and repair of mis-tuned control. **Candidate contributions:** C1 operating map of headroom against capacity-drop size; C2 calibrated replication of the BN4/Flow RL results; C3 adaptation gain of a learned scheduler for tuned ALINEA under imperfect detection and day mix; C4-C7 riskier or outside the line. Claude's recommendation: C1 + C2 as paper_1 (methods / replication), C3 as paper 2 if C1 shows headroom. A rate in the band can act only between 300 s and the onset: median 72 s on the planned tuning seeds and 153 s on the evaluation seeds. NC broke down before the onset on 1 of 20 V147 seeds. The BN4 plant **failed T1a** in V147 (60 % of seeds in range against 75 %), so 'realistic plant' in V149 and V154 is not established. **(3) Bug after results (CLAUDE.md rule):** the emergency-stop regex at `v5b/p1c/episode.py:31` expects `lane='...'`, but SUMO writes `on lane '...'`. Fix and re-derivation pending. **(4) Critic X1 (estimate, not verified):** a fluid estimate puts the ceiling of any mainline meter, RL included, in this cell at about 6-10 %, at or below the 10 % bar. Readings: (a) compute same-lane rear-end TTC ourselves every second from `getLeader`; SUMO's SSM runtime value `device.ssm.minTTC` is the minimum since the encounter began and also covers other-lane foes (libsumo 1.27.1 test: 19 times more seconds below 3 s); (b) TTC < 1.5 s is the most used threshold (SSAM default) but no freeway crash-validated threshold exists; 1.0 s and 3.0 s as sensitivity arms; (c) published crash-to-conflict ratios span 1:1,000 to 1:100,000 (mostly Swedish urban intersections), so a severity-weighted p (shape from an EVT tail fitted to no-control runs, one scale tuned to the target) is recommended, constant p as a sensitivity arm; (d) the V165 target (P(≥1 crash/day) = 25 %, λ 0.288/day) on about 1 km is 8-14 crashes per million veh-km against about 0.6 on US urban freeways (all reported crashes, Caltrans) and 0.028 injury crashes on GB motorways: a stress regime, to be labelled so; (e) 30-60 min blockage fits HCM incident defaults (means 35-68 min, US); 57 % of GB motorway injury collisions involve two vehicles; (f) UK motorway weekday profile (WebTRIS, 10 sites): peak hour 7.75 % of daily traffic, so about 18,000 veh/day puts the peak at 1,400 veh/h; (g) no study validates SUMO conflicts against freeway crashes | result |

Rule (`v5b/fleet/merge.py` RampMerge, all values assumptions): each whole second, a ramp vehicle that has stood (speed < 2 m/s) in the last 60 m of the acceleration lane for ≥ 10 s changes to the lane on its left with SUMO's safe-gap check skipped (TraCI lane-change mode bits 8-9 = 1, so only overlaps are avoided), when the left follower crawls (< 3 m/s) and the vehicle fits where it stands: its own minGap clear ahead, and behind it the follower's minGap plus the follower's stopping distance at its normal deceleration (so a space of at least car length + 2 × 2.5 m). Teleporting off in both arms, as on crash days. Test: PM 13:00-20:00 of the v190 reference day, ADT 45,000, seeds 7,430,040-049, paired arms squeeze on and off, 20 runs. Adopt the squeeze if, with it, all 10 seeds drain with 0 SUMO collisions and the median number of ramp vehicles waiting > 60 s is below the median without it. Otherwise option 2 (no squeeze, teleporting off), if the off arm drains with 0 collisions on all 10 seeds; if neither, stop for the author. Either way the plant then runs with teleporting off, and Layer A's 0-teleport check becomes: drained, 0 SUMO collisions, merge waits reported. Reported: squeezes and failed squeezes per PM, waits > 10 / 60 / 300 s and the longest, emergency braking, TTC < 1.5 s seconds, merge and lane-drop breakdowns, ramp queue, delays by class, paired differences | decided |

| V199 | 2026-10-30 | **Incident passing zone: design and calibration fixed before building** (author "Go" to the V198 next step). Target (Knoop, Hoogendoorn & Adams 2009, EJTIR 9(4), Table 5, read in full in V176): queue discharge at the incident site over the normal queue discharge F = 0.36 (SD 0.14) with 1 of 3 lanes blocked; efficiency of the remaining lanes η = F / (open lanes / lanes) = 0.54 with 1 or 2 of 3 blocked. Mechanism (`v5b/fleet/incident.py`; values are assumptions unless sourced): while a crash blocks a lane, human drivers (HDV cars and trucks; CAVs keep their gaps) within 100 m upstream (along the road, across edges) and 100 m downstream of the front crashed vehicle, on any lane, drive with their tau multiplied by f; set at whole seconds, restored when they leave the zone or the crash is removed; f = 1 is a no-op. One knob: speeds fall by themselves, a simplification of "longer headway and lower speed". Calibration: a straight 3-lane road (100 km/h, 5 km), the V187 human fleet with plant v188 driver knobs, constant demand 5,000 veh/h (above the 3,588 veh/h two lanes would discharge at full efficiency, below 3 × the V187 pre-queue flow 1,938); after 600 s the first human vehicle on the right lane to reach 3,000 m crashes alone (V163 braking and lane-change rule), blocked for 1,800 s. Discharge = vehicles over a loop 300 m downstream (all lanes) from 300 s after the crash to its removal; valid if the 60-s speed at a loop 500 m upstream is below 70 km/h (Knoop's congestion threshold; no vehicles counts as congested) in ≥ 90 % of the window. η = discharge / (2 × 1,794), with 1,794 veh/h the plant's per-lane queue discharge (V187). Grid f ∈ {1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5, 3.0, 4.0} × seeds 7,430,040-049 (60 runs). Choice: the f whose median η over valid seeds is closest to 0.54; it passes if that median is within 0.54 ± 0.05 with ≥ 8 valid seeds; if none passes but two values bracket 0.54, one refinement grid of three values between them; otherwise stop for the author. Report-only rows at the chosen f (10 seeds each): zone ± 300 m; crash in the left lane; a 2-lane road with 1 lane blocked (demand 3,300 veh/h; F about 0.27 from η, HCM 2010 0.35) at the chosen f and at 1.0. Adoption: plant v199 = v190 + the zone. Crash-free days are unchanged by construction; the 10 V198 crash-live days are re-run (9 hashes must match, the …041 first crash must match) and the crash day's delay effect is reported | decided |

Median η over valid seeds (95 % CI) on the 3-lane road, right lane blocked: f 1.0 (SUMO as is) 1.29 [1.28, 1.34], only 4 valid seeds because the open lanes discharged 4,626 veh/h and the queue barely formed; 1.5 0.97; 2.0 0.74; 2.5 0.62; **3.0 0.532 [0.529, 0.540], 10 valid, F 0.355 (field 0.36)**; 4.0 0.43. So SUMO alone lets the two open lanes run above the plant's normal queue discharge, and crashes were far too mild. | V209 | 2026-10-31 | **The dry-day collision of the V207 batch is a SUMO lane-change artefact** (stop rule of V207: 1 SUMO collision in the first 63 finished days, dry seed 7,431,093; author "yes" to a diagnostic replay). The replay with the frozen code `8d74cf5` reproduced it to the step and vehicle (t = 29,619.4-29,619.5 s, lane 3_1 at 797 m, about 400 m before the 3 → 2 lane drop). Sequence: an ALKS vehicle (IDM, tau 1.6 s) stopped at the end of the queue on lane 3_2, which ends at the lane drop, changed to 3_1 at 0.07 m/s (strategic, urgent) at t = 29,619.1, leaving about 0.15 m (beyond minGap) to the HDV car behind it; that car (Krauss, tau 0.549 s, actionStepLength 0.5 s, the V187 calibrated action step) had stood still with its leader 7.4 m ahead and at its action point t = 29,619.0 chose +2.56 m/s²; it could not react before its next action point and reached 1.28 m/s before braking. **No physical contact** (corrected the same day from "struck … overlap 0.07 m"): the closest bumper-to-bumper distance was 2.36 m (t = 29,619.7 s), i.e. the car entered the 2.5 m minGap by up to 0.15 m. SUMO 1.27.1 flags that as a collision here because `--collision.mingap-factor` is left at its default (the car-following model's collisionMinGapFactor), so every earlier "0 collisions" check also counted minGap intrusions. LC2013 judged the gap safe from the follower's current speed, not from its planned acceleration. Neither the passing zone, the weather code nor the ramp merge acts there. The batch keeps running; the acceptance rule (strategy row A, `docs/research/weather_plant_strategy_v0.md`) goes to the author | result |

| V211 | 2026-10-31 | **Collision rule: physical contacts and minGap intrusions counted apart** (author "A OK" to Claude's recommendation after V209). A SUMO collision event is a physical contact unless its bumper-to-bumper distance stayed above 0 over all its steps; an event without a distance counts as contact. Any contact keeps the V207 stop rule (stop for the author). minGap intrusions are counted, reported per weather state and per controller arm, and disclosed; an arm whose intrusion rate is above no control's (paired CI excluding 0) gets a safety flag and its cells are not claimed without the author. SUMO options are unchanged (`--collision.mingap-factor` default), so dynamics and hashes are unchanged. Code: `day._bumper` (collider front to victim back = getLeader distance + collider minGap, the formula checked on the V209 trace) per collision step, `_first_steps` keeps the event's smallest distance, `contact_counts` adds `contacts` and `mingap_intrusions` to every day result; `tests/test_fleet_collisions.py`. Runs from frozen code before this commit (the V207 batch) record speeds only; their events are settled by replay (V209: the one dry event is an intrusion, 2.36 m). Rejected: counting only contacts in SUMO (`--collision.mingap-factor 0`; loses the near-miss record), counting every SUMO event as a collision (treats a 2.4 m near-miss as a crash), new lane-change parameters or a shorter human action step (re-runs V187-V207 and breaks the only passing V187 configuration), a CAV lane-end gap rule (unsourced, re-runs every mixed-fleet result), dropping the day (seed cherry-picking). V207 phase 2 stop-rule fields: 90/90 days drained, 0 teleports, 0 SUMO errors, 1 event (the V209 intrusion) | decided |

(5) O_off could count after a flagged tuned pass under Rule 2; it now needs no tuned pass at all (§9.5). (6) Post-hoc power used the 10-pair s_U at n = 20; now n pairs. (7) Gain-check RMS paired each active minute with the occupancy one minute early; corrected; choices unchanged (ALINEA K_R 70: RMS 10.20 vs 10.84 and 11.73; MTFC K_I 0.02). (8) A failed attempt cut short by a stop is now kept (ATTEMPT1_FAILED.txt; one attempt on resume). (9) `day batch --forced` works again. (10) Noise floors and the global null took 12 points and 6 per family; `e1 report --wave floors` recomputes them at the frozen grid. (11) Disclosure: the T0 rate check pools 5 clean minutes, not necessarily adjacent (the code's reading of '5-min interval' since V220); the clock-interval values are now reported too (`ratio_clock`). The n = 20 R result is **inconclusive**: neither a null nor a 5-8 % effect. (1) ALINEA 1.05 is +8.3 % [-0.5, +15.1] on the slice (mean version +5.2 %, 14/20 seeds; sign test p 0.058 one-sided, unadjusted). If ALINEA had no effect (MTFC as observed), the best arm reaches ≥ +8.3 % with P 0.21-0.23, so the data fit 'no effect' and '+8-10 %' about equally well. A true +10 % is not excluded for any ALINEA point. Distribution-free upper bounds reach about +18 % per arm and about +22 % for the family. Full day is about +7 % (upper about +13 %, × 0.858, approximate). The 0.85 L1 → L2 reversal is ordinary n = 10 noise (exact split test P 0.62); 10-seed tuning is optimistic by about +0.06 (cross-fit +0.024). (2) ALINEA 1.05's gain is a net mainline gain (+2,310 veh·h) partly paid for by ramp drivers (-575 veh·h). The breakdown does not shift (median t_b +7 min [-58.5, +38.5]). In 16-19 h the ALINEA law sets the rate in only 0.6-2.8 % of minutes; queue override or backstop sets it in 72.5-78.9 % (inference: the meter has little feedback authority in the jam). (3) Plant (inference): the PM queue is demand-driven (16-17 h demand 3,508 veh/h against a queue discharge of 3,141, +11.7 %). On these slices the protocol's capacity-drop statistic (9.0-9.6 %) sits at the level of in-queue noise (13.8 %; null simulation about 15 %). A perfectly timed meter limited to 4-min waits could recover only about 1-5 % by postponing the breakdown; that bound covers that channel only. (4) MTFC is robustly worse for this configuration (Bonferroni × 9 upper bounds -0.079 to -0.172): b sits at b_min in 56-61 % of the slice, it restricts flow before the breakdown, the breakdown starts 10-45 min earlier and lasts 47-69 min longer, and discharge is 1.8-2.7 % lower. b is clamped, so there is no windup. (5) Not shown: no-effect, a ceiling, anything about the I cell, other demand, rain, MC*, or CAV control in general. Untested: the upper grid edges (ALINEA 1.15 and MTFC long 1.15) and MC*. The 4-min wait breaches come mostly from the plant (39.8 % of NC ramp trips already exceed 240 s of delay). |

D5.10's gain over no control (-49.3 %) rests on avoiding the 900 veh/h standing state: a constant 1,800 veh/h meter ties the DRL in the unperturbed regime (-0.79 s [-3.19, 2.86]), and all three R6+ learners have converged_c1 = false. (3) **P1/P1b and C1** ran on the same plant (identical net and plant code at 85f7d34, 93ba655 and e7dd561). C1 (V116): about half of the IP - PI gap is drain at 900 veh/h; in the window, IP vs PI is 11.7 % [6.5, 14.4], but PI is worse than no control by 5.7 % on window TTT, so it is a weak opponent. (4) **V149's 'realistic 5-8 % drop'** was a max-based statistic on zipper + Krauss at 0.5 s, whose QDF equals SUMO's zipper cap at 0.5 s (already noted in V181 and not acted on) and is step-dependent. The run would test "credit from per-vehicle terms only", not variance reduction, and the shared term carries delay, the side v3w loses on (V385: 33-45 % of the reward's magnitude). The unbiased alternative (leave-one-out across the other envs only) is weak with 3 envs at different demand levels. The branch stays as a record; rep-slow is built next. |
